The NYISO proposes the following limited exceptions to this restriction on modifications 
during the Large Load Integration Study Process.  The Eligible Customer may request a 
modification for its Large Load Project: (i) to reduce the project size in the SIS Entry Decision 
Period or the ALI Entry Decision Period, provided that the project with the reduced size 
continues to satisfy the definition of Large Load Project, (ii) to modify the Point of 
Interconnection for the project to address Physical Infeasibility in the SIS Entry Decision Period 
or in the ALI Entry Decision Period, provided that the modification is agreed upon with the 
Connecting Transmission Owner and does not modify the electrical characteristics of the project, 
or (iii) to include or change the Associated Resource(s) that satisfy the Associated Resource 
requirements in the ALI Entry Decision Period.151  This set of modifications will provide 
Eligible Customers with a limited opportunity to modify their projects in response to preliminary 
input in the Initial Study Phase.  The permitted modifications will generally reduce or lessen the 
severity of the reliability issues identified for the project, which minimizes the potential impact 
of such modifications on other projects participating in the study. 
c) 
Material Modifications  
Notwithstanding the modification review process, the NYISO proposes that the following 
modifications will be considered a Material Modification and require the submission of a Large 
Load Integration Request under the Large Load Integration Procedures.152 
First, the NYISO proposes to establish certain requirements concerning modifications to 
existing loads that are already integrated into the transmission system.  Large existing loads in 
New York are primarily traditional manufacturing load that have been managed for years with a 
good understanding of their potential reliability impacts.  Accordingly, the NYISO proposes to 
only evaluate modifications to existing load in the Large Load Integration Procedures if a 
modification has the potential for significant impacts to the Bulk Electric System.  For an 
existing load that is in service prior to June 18, 2026, the NYISO proposes that an increase in the 
peak, gross load amount by 50 MW or greater compared to the baseline MW value (as reported 
by the Connecting Transmission Owner) will be a Material Modification and require study in the 
Large Load Integration Study Process.  If, however, such existing load proposes to change its 
load type and thus its operational characteristics (e.g., changing from a manufacturer to a data 
center), the NYISO proposes that a smaller increase in the peak, gross load amount by 10 MW or 
greater compared to the baseline MW value (as reported by the Connecting Transmission 
Owner) would constitute a Material Modification so that the NYISO can assess the reliability 
impacts of this change. 
Second, for load projects participating in Transition Group A and for subsequent Large 
Load Projects participating in the Transition Large Load Integration Study Process or a Large 
Load Integration Study Process, the NYISO proposes that an increase in the peak, gross load 
 
151 Proposed OATT Attach. LL § 44.7.2.2.1. 
152 Id. § 44.7.2.1.1.

PDF page 39

Hon. Debbie-Anne A. Reese

amount by 10 MW or greater compared to the baseline MW value will constitute a Material 
Modification.153  The Large Load Project is permitted to increase its size less than 10 MW 
without such modification being a Material Modification.  However, this 10 MW amount is 
determined on a cumulative basis.  For example, if a 100 MW load seeks to increase its size by 5 
MW, such modification will not be a Material Modification.  If, however, the Large Load seeks a 
second 5 MW increase that brings its cumulative increase from the initial 100 MW to 110 MW, 
this second increase would constitute a Material Modification.  
Finally, the NYISO proposes that any change to the Point of Interconnection for an 
existing load or a Large Load Project is a Material Modification, except as permitted during the 
SIS Entry Decision Period or ALI Entry Decision Period as described above. 
vii. 
Withdrawal 
The NYISO proposes to establish withdrawal requirements for the Large Load 
Integration Procedures.154  In particular, an Eligible Customer may withdraw its Large Load 
Integration Request at any time by submitting written notice of such withdrawal to the 
NYISO.155  Further, if an Eligible Customer fails to adhere to all requirements of these Large 
Load Integration Procedures, the NYISO will deem the Large Load Integration Request to be 
withdrawn and shall provide written notice to the Eligible Customer of the withdrawal and an 
explanation of the reasons for such withdrawal.156 
The NYISO proposes that the Eligible Customer have a cure period of 15 Business Days 
in which to either respond with information or actions that cures the deficiency or to notify the 
NYISO of its intent to pursue Dispute Resolution.157  The NYISO, however, proposes that 
certain time-critical deadlines will not be eligible for a cure period.158  This will reduce the 
potential for study delays that adversely impact all Large Load Projects participating in the 
study.  This is consistent with the withdrawal requirements in the NYISO’s Standard 
Interconnection Procedures.159   
The withdrawal of a Large Load Project will result in the loss of the Eligible Customer’s 
Load Queue Position.160  An Eligible Customer that withdraws or is deemed to have withdrawn

G. 
Assessment of Reliability Impacts and Determination of Withdrawal Service 
i. 
Determination of Withdrawal Service  
The NYISO proposes to determine the amounts of Firm Withdrawal Service and Non-
Firm Withdrawal Service for each Large Load Project and, if requested, to determine eligibility 
to be classified as Zero Withdrawal Service Load, based on both the resource adequacy and 
transmission security impacts of the project and available actions to mitigate any adverse 
reliability impacts.164  This approach ensures the reliability of the New York State Transmission 
system and that Large Loads will be responsible for the costs associated with mitigating any 
adverse impacts to system reliability resulting from their interconnection.  
For purposes of assessing the reliability impacts of the proposed interconnection of a 
Large Load Project, the NYISO proposes to establish a Resource Adequacy Impact Standard (as 
described in Part II.G.ii) and a Transmission Security Standard (as described in Part 
II.G.iii).  The NYISO proposes that the analyses be applied to ensure that New York State 
Transmission System facilities are sufficient to reliably serve existing load and meet load growth 
and changes in load patterns in compliance with Applicable Reliability Requirements.165  These 
requirements include  New York State Reliability Council (“NYSRC”) Reliability Rules, 
Northeast Power Coordinating Council (“NPCC”) Basic Design and Operating Criteria, North 
American Electric Reliability Corporation (“NERC”) Planning Standards, NYISO rules, 
practices and procedures, and the applicable Transmission Owner criteria included in FERC 
Form No. 715.166  These Applicable Reliability Requirements are consistent with the NYISO’s 
requirements for its Standard Interconnection Procedures.167  The NYISO and Transmission 
Owners, as applicable, will apply the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard and Transmission 
 
161 Proposed OATT Attach. LL § 44.7.3.2.

Owner, and Affected Transmission Owner before it is allowed to obtain any Large Load Integration Study Process 
data or results.  Id. § 44.7.3.2.

Security Standard in line with these Applicable Reliability Requirements in the appropriate 
studies in the Large Load Integration Study Process as described below. 
If there are no adverse impacts or violations, as applicable, identified in accordance with 
the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard or Transmission Security Standard as described below, 
the Large Load Project may proceed with Firm Withdrawal Service.   
a) 
Addressing Adverse Impacts Under Resource Adequacy Impact 
Standard 
If an adverse impact is identified under the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard, a Large 
Load Project may mitigate the adverse impacts through its association with an Associated 
Resource and remain eligible for Firm Withdrawal Service.168  In such case, the Large Load 
Project will obtain Non-Firm Withdrawal Service until a Large Load Bilateral Transaction with 
an Associated Resource, as part of a Co-Located Resource Arrangement or Partner Resource 
Arrangement, is initially certified.169  Alternatively, the Large Load Project may elect to proceed 
with Non-Firm Withdrawal Service (in full or part) without proposing an association with  an 
Associated Resource.

must be a proposed project that does not yet meet the inclusion rules for the Reliability Planning

For all arrangements between a Large Load and an Associated Resource, the 
arrangements must satisfy all applicable requirements for a term of 15 years and have a Large

NYISO administered markets or the arrangement otherwise fails to comply with applicable 
requirements prior to the end of the 15-year term, then the Large Load ceases to be eligible for 
the amount of Firm Withdrawal Service that depends on the arrangement with the Associated 
Resource, as further detailed under the proposed provisions in the Services Tariff and the 
OATT.174  The Associated Resource eligibility and ongoing requirements are further discussed 
below in Part IV.F. 
b) 
Addressing Violations Under Transmission Security Standard 
If a violation is identified under the Transmission Security Standard, a Large Load 
Project may either take Non-Firm Withdrawal Service or may proceed with Network Upgrades 
that mitigate the violation.175  In such case, the Large Load Project will obtain Non-Firm 
Withdrawal Service until the Network Upgrades are complete and in service, after which the 
Large Load Project will obtain Firm Withdrawal Service.176  Notwithstanding the impact on 
Withdrawal Service, certain transmission security violations cannot be mitigated by the project 
taking Non-Firm Withdrawal Service and require Network Upgrades (as described in Part II.G.iii 
below).  For these upgrades that are necessary for the project to obtain Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service or, if requested, to be eligible to be classified as a Zero Withdrawal Service Load, the 
Large Load Project may not enter service until such Network Upgrades are complete and in 
service or the NYISO and applicable Transmission Owner determine that such reliability impacts 
can be managed through normal operating procedures until the Network Upgrades are 
complete.177  These Withdrawal Service alternatives are illustrated in the following figure:

c) 
Withdrawal Service Based on Results Under Both Resource 
Adequacy Impact Standard and Transmission Security Standard 
The NYISO’s final determination concerning Withdrawal Service will be based on both 
the results of the analyses using the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard and the Transmission 
Security Standard and any mitigation actions concerning adverse impacts or violations identified 
based on either of these standards.  For example, a Large Load Project for which there are no 
adverse impacts on resource adequacy may still be limited to Non-Firm Withdrawal Service if 
there are transmission security violations that are not mitigated in the manner described above.    
d) 
LIPA Exception Regarding Non-Firm Withdrawal Service 
The NYISO proposes to include in its Withdrawal Service requirements, to the extent 
accepted by the Commission, the following exception requested by the Long Island Power 
Authority concerning the application of Non-Firm Withdrawal Service for projects 
interconnecting in the Long Island Transmission District.  In particular, Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service and the procedures for studying Non-Firm Withdrawal Service under the Large Load 
Integration Procedures for the Long Island Transmission District will only apply if dispatch 
procedures applicable to serving a Large Load on a non-firm basis have been adopted by the 
Board of Trustees for the Long Island Power Authority in accordance with Article 5, Title 1-A of 
the New York Public Authorities Law.178  The Long Island Power Authority requested this 
exception based on its status as a non-jurisdictional utility under the Federal Power Act and will 
provide its more detailed justification for this proposed exception in a separate filing. 
ii. 
Resource Adequacy Impact Standard  
The NYISO proposes to establish a Resource Adequacy Impact Standard to evaluate the 
impact of each Large Load Project on the resource adequacy of the New York Control Area 
using probabilistic evaluations consistent with the Applicable Reliability Requirements, 
including the NYSRC Reliability Rules.179  This analysis will enable the NYISO to determine 
whether the Large Load Project proposing to interconnect to the New York State Transmission 
System: (i) is eligible to obtain Firm Withdrawal Service or (ii) due to the resource adequacy 
impacts, may either proceed with such service with an Associated Resource or is only eligible to

The NYISO proposes to apply this Resource Adequacy Impact Standard in the Resource 
Adequacy Evaluation performed during the RAMP Part 1 Process as described in Part 
II.H.iv.b.180  The NYISO will then perform this assessment anew in the Assessment of Load 
Integration as described in Part II.I.i and at any point thereafter as part of a review of a Large 
Load Project’s requested modification as described in Part II.F.vi, in each case if the Large Load 
Project modifies its load or includes or modifies any Associated Resources.181 
For purposes of this assessment, the NYISO proposes to use the most recent resource 
adequacy model from the Reliability Planning Process, as updated in accordance with the 
NYISO’s procedures.182  The NYISO will test a Large Load together with any Associated 
Resource(s), with the assumption that the Associated Resource(s) will obtain Capacity Resource 
Interconnection Service.183  The NYISO will consider the operating characteristics of the Large

The amounts of Firm Withdrawal Service and Non-Firm Withdrawal Service determined

actions to mitigate any adverse reliability impacts as described above.185 
a) 
Resource Adequacy Impact Standard Except for State Exempt Large 
Load Projects  
The NYISO proposes to apply the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard differently 
depending on whether or not a Large Load Project is identified by the NYDPS as a State Exempt 
Large Load Project as described below.  The NYISO proposes to apply the Resource Adequacy 
Impact Standard in the manner described: (i) in this Part II.G.ii.a for Large Load Projects that are 
not identified as State Exempt Large Load Projects and (ii) in Part II.G.ii.b for State Exempt 
Large Load Projects. 
 
The NYISO proposes to apply the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard in the following 
manner to determine the amount of Non-Firm Withdrawal Service and Firm Withdrawal Service 
that the Large Load Project is eligible for based on resource adequacy impacts to the New York 
Control Area.186  The NYISO will evaluate a Large Load Project for years 1 through 10 of the 
study to determine the extent that the project degrades resource adequacy in consideration of any 
offset by an Associated Resource.187  If the Large Load Project degrades resource adequacy by

25 MW or less, the Large Load Project will be eligible for Firm Withdrawal Service in its full 
requested amount, provided that it completes any identified Network Upgrades to address any 
violations of the Transmission Security Standard.188  If the Large Load Project degrades resource 
adequacy by greater than 25 MW, the Large Load Project will be eligible for a partial amount of

of the Withdrawal Service must be Non-Firm Withdrawal Service to bring the Loss of Load

As described in the Altman Affidavit, the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard is just and 
reasonable because it provides a clear requirement for the integration of Large Load Projects to 
not adversely impact NYCA resource adequacy and, thus, reliability.191  The use of the Resource 
Adequacy Impact Standard is particularly important when the NYISO faces statewide resource 
deficiencies, which it has recently identified beginning in 2033 in its current longer-term 
reliability planning assessment.192  In applying the 25 MW impact threshold, the NYISO will 
employ a probabilistic evaluation comparable to those used in evaluating resource adequacy 
under the rules established by NYSRC and calculate the Large Load Project’s MW impact.193  
The proposed methodology that the NYISO will use to calculate the 25 MW impact threshold is 
also reasonable and consistent with the NYISO’s longstanding consideration of similar impacts 
when studying proposed interconnections to the New York State Transmission System.194  Using 
a threshold, as opposed to a precise calculation, provides a reasonable tolerance commensurate 
with the degree of precision in the models and avoids potentially subjecting Large Load Projects 
to adverse findings based on the modeling that are immaterial to a project’s impact to 
reliability.195

Resource Adequacy Impact Standard for State Exempt Large Load

The NYISO proposes to establish a category of Large Load Projects – State Exempt 
Large Load Projects – that will be subject to an alternative Resource Adequacy Impact Standard.

Large Load Project through an exemption process adopted by the NYPSC in its ongoing large 
load interconnection proceedings.196  The Eligible Customer must provide this verification as 
part of either the project’s submission of its Large Load Integration Request or during the ALI 
Entry Decision Period.197

remaining State Exempt Large Load Projects violate the resource adequacy criterion contained in 
the NYSRC Reliability Rules for years 1 through 5 of the study.  If the collective impact of the 
remaining State Exempt Large Load Projects does not violate the resource adequacy criterion

c) 
The State Exempt Large Load Projects Proposal Is Consistent with 
Cooperative Federalism 
The NYPSC stated in the Show Cause Order proceeding that the FPA gives it the  
primary role in ensuring the adequacy of generation resources in New York, and that it intends to 
exercise its authority with respect to large load resource adequacy.203 Meanwhile, the 
Commission has jurisdiction over resource adequacy issues to the extent that they affect 
wholesale electricity market, interstate transmission, or bulk power system reliability matters.204  
The Commission’s interest in these types of resource adequacy issues is reflected in the Show 
Cause Order, including its informational report directive and its suggestion that the NYISO take 
other steps to address resource adequacy challenges.205  And the NYISO itself has resource 
adequacy responsibilities under applicable NERC, NPCC, and NYSRC requirements and its 
Commission-jurisdictional tariffs.   
 
Thus, resource adequacy implicates both federal and state jurisdiction.  Consistent with 
the Show Cause Order,206 the NYISO has designed the State Exempt Large Load Projects 
proposal to respect cooperative federalism.  As the Altman Affidavit explains,207 the NYISO 
needs to consider the resource adequacy implications of Firm Withdrawal Service awards in 
order to fulfill its reliability obligations.  The NYISO is not seeking to displace New York State’s 
resource adequacy authority or “targeting” state-jurisdictional activity.208  The NYISO is simply 
accounting for resource adequacy implications when deciding whether to make Commission-
jurisdictional transmission service available to Commission-jurisdictional Transmission 
Customers.  At the same time, the NYISO’s proposal would preserve state authority by enabling 
the NYDPS to determine that certain Large Loads should be allowed to receive firm service even 
if they would otherwise individually exceed the NYISO’s Resource Adequacy Impact Standard.  
Making this treatment unavailable to the extent that State Exempt Large Load Projects would 
collectively cause a resource adequacy violation is not an impermissible intrusion into state 
jurisdiction.  It is a reasonable safeguard to ensure that federal reliability requirements are 
maintained. 
 
 
203 See Notice of Intervention and Preliminary Comment of the New York State Public Service 
Commission, Docket No. EL26-69-000 (July 9, 2026) (“NYPSC Preliminary Comments”) at 3-5. 
204 See, e.g., Conn. Dept. of Pub. Util. Control v. FERC, 569 F.3d 477, 485 (D.C. Cir. 2009) (distinguishing 
between  “direct regulation of generation facilities” — which is beyond the Commission’s reach — and 
determination of the installed capacity requirement, which directly “affects rates within the Commission’s 
jurisdiction.”) 
205 See Show Cause Order at P 128.  
206 See id. at P 76 (“Indeed, the Commission’s objective in this proceeding is to respect the cooperative 
federalism approach established under the FPA”); NYPSC Preliminary Comments at 4-5 (acknowledging this 
statement and the Commission’s commitment to cooperative federalism.)  
207 Altman Affidavit at PP 8-10, 37-38. 
208 In FERC v. Elec. Power Supply Ass’n, 577 U.S. 260, 264 (2016) (“EPSA”) and ONEOK, Inc. v. Learjet, 
Inc., 575 U.S. 373, 385 (2015), the Supreme Court was clear that whether a particular rule “targeted” federal or state 
matters was a key factor in determining whether the rule was within the Commission’s jurisdiction.

The NYISO’s balanced approach is permissible under the FPA.  The Supreme Court has 
clearly established that “the wholesale and retail markets in electricity…are not hermetically 
sealed from each other.”209  There is room for reasonable compromise210 in areas where federal 
and state jurisdiction overlap.  In this case, the proposed Resource Adequacy Impact Standard is 
critical to the NYISO’s ability to serve Large Loads. The State Exempt Large Load Project 
proposal, therefore, represents a program of cooperative federalism and recognizes New York 
State’s role regarding resource adequacy without encroaching upon federal jurisdiction over 
wholesale electricity rates and transmission service.  
 
iii. 
Transmission Security Standard 
The NYISO proposes to establish a Transmission Security Standard to evaluate the 
impact of each Large Load Project on the transmission security of the New York Control Area as 
described below and in the Nguyen Affidavit.211  The NYISO proposes that the Transmission 
Security Standard be applied to assess the reliability impacts of the proposed interconnection of 
the proposed Large Load Project and determine the amount of Firm Withdrawal Service and 
Non-Firm Withdrawal Service that the Large Load Project is eligible for and, if requested, 
whether the Large Load Project, or any portion, is eligible to be classified as a Zero Withdrawal 
Service Load based on transmission security impacts.212  The amounts of Firm Withdrawal 
Service and Non-Firm Withdrawal Service determined using the Transmission Security Standard 
will be subject to modification based on: (i) the determinations using the Resource Adequacy 
Impact Standard and (ii) available actions to mitigate any adverse reliability impacts, as 
described above.213 
The NYISO proposes that this assessment be used in the System Impact Study.214  In 
addition, the NYISO proposes that this assessment then be performed: (i) anew in the 
Assessment of Load Integration and (ii) at any point thereafter as part of a review of a Large 
Load Project’s requested modification if the Large Load Project modifies its load or includes or 
modifies a Co-Located Resource(s).215  For purposes of this assessment, the NYISO proposes 
that the year 5 cases of the most recent quarterly Short-Term Assessment of Reliability 
(“STAR”) base case in the Short-Term Reliability Process be used to evaluate the impact of each 
Large Load Project on the transmission security of the New York Control Area.216  The Large 
Load will be tested together with any Associated Resource, as applicable, and the operating

classified as a Zero Withdrawal Service Load, the violation must be addressed by one or more 
Network Upgrades.  Violations that cannot be resolved through receiving Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service include any potential adverse reliability impact that degrades system reliability or results 
in noncompliance with the NERC, NPCC, or NYSRC reliability standards and that cannot be 
managed through the normal operating procedures of the NYISO and/or Transmission Owner.227

i. 
Overview of New Study Process 
In the Show Cause Order, the Commission indicated that the NYISO’s tariff appears to 
lack sufficiently clear and consistent provisions establishing a study process for evaluating the 
provision of transmission service to Eligible Customers on behalf of large loads that considers 
the unique characteristics and impacts of large loads on the transmission system, including 
identifying the Direct Assignment Facilities and Network Upgrades necessary to provide such 
service reliably and providing a non-binding good-faith estimate of the cost and the time to 
construct such facilities.228  The Commission further indicated that the NYISO’s tariff may not 
clearly and consistently require the NYISO or the Transmission Owners to conduct studies that 
evaluate the transmission system’s ability to withstand risks observed from analyzed large load 
behavior created by the large load at the point of interconnection, or any other necessary 
supplemental studies to evaluate the impact of providing transmission service to Eligible 
Customers on behalf of large loads.229 
The NYISO’s existing load interconnection procedures in Sections 3.9 and 4.5 of the 
OATT provide for it, in cooperation with the applicable Transmission Owner, to perform 
technical studies to determine whether the proposed interconnection may degrade system 
reliability or adversely affect the operation of the NYS Power System.230  The NYISO proposes 
to establish in place of these requirements a new Large Load Integration Study Process in 
Attachment LL of the OATT.  This process will include detailed, clear and consistent 
requirements for the evaluation of the impacts of the proposed interconnection of Large Load 
Projects in a manner that accounts for the unique operational characteristic of Large Loads, 
including identifying any Attachment Facilities or Network Upgrades required for the reliable 
interconnection of the Large Load and determining the type and amount of Withdrawal Service 
for the Large Load Project.   
In particular, the NYISO proposes an Initial Study Phase as described in this Part II.H in 
which the NYISO and Transmission Owners will perform certain initial analyses of the proposed 
interconnection and a System Impact Study.  These collectively will provide a Large Load 
Project with a preliminary assessment of the feasibility of its proposed interconnection and 
potential Attachment Facilities and Network Upgrades, including preliminary estimates of the 
costs and time to construct such facilities.  If an Eligible Customer elects to proceed with its 
Large Load Project, the NYISO will then perform an Assessment of Load Integration as 
described in Part II.I.i in parallel with the Transmission Owner’s performance of a Facilities 
Study as described in Part II.I.ii to identify both the required Attachment Facilities and Network 
Upgrades and to establish the final determination of Withdrawal Service that the project is 
eligible to obtain. 
 
228 Show Cause Order at PP 66, 69. 
229 Id. at P 66.  The Show Cause Order recognized the Transmission Owners may be partially responsible 
for conducting the studies that evaluate whether a large load may be interconnected to the transmission system and 
served by the Eligible Customer and that determine whether Network Upgrades are necessary to provide 
transmission service.  Id. at 58. 
230 OATT §§ 3.9.1, 3.9.2; NYISO Manual 23, Transmission Expansion and Interconnection Manual § 3.5 
(Issued: February 2025).

Page 51 
 
 
The NYISO proposes to perform the Large Load Integration Study Process on a clustered 
basis for a Group of Large Load Projects that have satisfied the application requirements 
described in Part II.F to enter the next available Group, rather than performing such study 
through serial, individual studies.231  As with the NYISO’s Cluster Study Process in its Standard 
Interconnection Procedures, which evaluates a cluster of Cluster Study Projects, it is just and 
reasonable for the NYISO to perform the Large Load Integration Study Process on a clustered 
basis.  As the Commission found in Order No. 2023 in adopting a cluster study process for 
generator interconnection requests, a group study increases efficiencies as the transmission 
provider can perform larger interconnection studies encompassing many proposed facilities, 
rather than individual studies for each customer.232  As described in the Nguyen Affidavit, a 
grouped study permits the NYISO and the Transmission Owners to evaluate the collective 
reliability effects of Large Load additions and to identify coordinated upgrades, rather than 
conducting overlapping serial studies that could produce duplicative or inconsistent solutions.233  
Although the addition of multiple projects and larger aggregate MW quantities increases the 
technical complexity of the study, the grouped approach provides a more comprehensive basis 
for assigning responsibility for shared impacts and allocating the costs of common Network 
Upgrades. 
The NYISO proposes that the study work in the Large Load Integration Study Process 
will be allocated between the NYISO and the Transmission Owners as detailed in this filing 
letter and as described in the Nguyen Affidavit.234  This approach reasonably allocates the study 
work to the entity with the relevant system knowledge, planning responsibility, and access to the 
underlying criteria and data.  The parallel division of work will also allow the NYISO and the 
Transmission Owners, working in parallel, to meet the proposed study timeframes and minimize 
inefficient and duplicative analyses.  The NYISO will perform the evaluation of the impacts of 
the proposed interconnections of Large Load Projects on the Bulk Electric System portion of the 
New York State Transmission System and identify any violations and adverse impacts on the 
Bulk Electric System caused by the project.  In particular, the NYISO will evaluate impacts on 
the Bulk Electric System to provide a consistent statewide assessment of bulk-system reliability 
and interregional effects.  If more than one project contributes to the violation, then the NYISO 
will provide the Transmission Owners with the projects’ respective contributions to the identified 
violations to facilitate the allocation of upgrade costs. 
The applicable Transmission Owners will perform parallel evaluations for the impacts of 
the Large Load Projects to the non-Bulk Electric System.  Transmission Owners will evaluate 
impacts on their non-Bulk Electric System and distribution facilities because they maintain the 
detailed models, equipment information, local planning criteria, operating procedures, and design 
standards needed to identify localized impacts and develop practicable upgrades.  Each 
Transmission Owner will be responsible for identifying: (i) violations on the non-Bulk Electric

the Transmission Owner may perform a Facility Study on a grouped basis.  Id. § 44.12.1.3.

(iv) the allocation of costs among the Large Load Projects participating in the study process, 
based on the NYISO’s and Transmission Owner’s determinations of each project’s respective 
contribution to the identified violations, (v) Attachment Facilities, and (vi) estimates of the costs 
and time to construct the Attachment Facilities.     
ii. 
Initial Study Phase Overview 
The NYISO proposes to commence the Large Load Integration Study Process with an 
Initial Study Phase.235  This phase will consist of (i) the Resource Adequacy and Modeling 
Period as described in Part II.H.iii and (ii) the System Impact Study as described in Part 
II.H.vii.236  The NYISO proposes to perform up to two successive, independent rounds of the 
Initial Study Phase for each Large Load Integration Study Process cycle, which rounds will be 
referred to as Initial Study Round 1 and Initial Study Round 2.237  Performing two rounds will 
increase the number of Large Load Projects that may proceed to be evaluated in the Assessment 
of Load Integration for a given study cycle once that assessment is completed for the prior cycle.  
For Initial Study Round 1, the Resource Adequacy and Modeling Period and the System Impact 
Study will be performed for the Large Load Projects in Group 1, and for Initial Study Round 2, 
the Resource Adequacy and Modeling Period and the System Impact Study will be performed for 
the Large Load Projects in Group 2.238  The NYISO proposes to commence the Initial Study 
Phase and the Initial Study Round 1 on the Large Load Integration Study Process Start Date, 
which is described in Part II.E.i.239  Following the completion of the draft System Impact Study 
Report for Group 1 in the Initial Study Round 1, the NYISO will commence the Initial Study 
Round 2.240 
Notwithstanding the above, for the Transition Large Load Integration Study Process, the 
NYISO proposes that the Initial Study Phase be performed in a single round for the Group of 
entities in Transition Group B that satisfied the requirements to enter the transition study.  The 
Initial Study Phase for the transition process will only include certain elements of the Resource 
Adequacy and Modeling Period as the participants in the transition process were already 
evaluated in a system impact study and will not be subject to a new System Impact Study under 
the Attachment LL rules.  The Resource Adequacy and Modeling Period for the Transition Large 
Load Integration Study Process will commence on the Transition Large Load Integration Study 
Process Start Date, which is described in Part II.E.i above.241

The NYISO proposes that the Resource Adequacy and Modeling Period consist of the 
following three components: (i) the RAMP Part 1 Process as described in Part II.H.iv, (ii) the 
SIS Entry Decision Period as described in Part II.H.v, and (iii) the RAMP Part 2 Process as 
described in Part II.H.vi.242  The NYISO proposes that the scheduled duration of the Resource 
Adequacy and Modeling Period for each round in the Large Load Integration Study Process be a 
90 Calendar Day period between the start date and the NYISO’s presentation of the scope of the 
System Impact Study to stakeholders.243  For the Transition Large Load Integration Study 
Process for which there is no System Impact Study related work, the NYISO proposes a shorter 
period of 60 Calendar Day between the start date and the ALI Entry Decision Period.244 
iv. 
RAMP Part 1 Process  
The NYISO proposes that the RAMP Part 1 Process include the following elements to 
develop information for the System Impact Study and provide the Eligible Customer with initial 
information concerning its proposed interconnection.245  In particular, the NYISO will develop 
the base case for the System Impact Study and will perform a Resource Adequacy Evaluation for 
each Large Load Project.  In addition, the Connecting Transmission Owner will determine 
preliminary Attachment Facilities for each Large Load Project, and the Connecting Transmission 
Owner and any Affected Transmission Owners will perform a Physical Infeasibility 
Screening.246  The NYISO proposes that the RAMP Part 1 Process have a scheduled duration of 
60 Calendar Days.247 
a) 
Transmission Owner Responsibilities in the RAMP Part 1 Process 
The NYISO proposes to establish a Physical Infeasibility Screening in the Large Load 
Integration Study Process by which the Connecting Transmission Owner and any Affected 
Transmission Owner will review the proposed interconnection of each Large Load Project to 
assess whether the proposed Point of Interconnection is Physically Infeasible.248  This process 
step is consistent with the Physical Infeasibility Screening requirements for the Cluster Study 
Process in the NYISO’s Standard Interconnection Procedures.  It serves to catch early in the 
study process, when possible, those projects that will not be able to interconnect due to physical 
infeasibility, so that such projects can withdraw without incurring or causing significant costs, 
time, and resources.  The NYISO and Transmission Owners have successfully applied this 
process in the NYISO’s Cluster Study Process to facilitate either the modification of Points of 
Interconnection, where permitted, or the withdrawal of projects that would not otherwise have 
been able to interconnect at their requested Point of Interconnection.249

Page 54 
 
 
The NYISO proposes to define Physically Infeasible in Attachment LL consistent with its 
definition in the Standard Interconnection Procedures in Attachment HH.250  In particular, a 
Large Load Integration Request will be Physically Infeasible if: 
(1) the substation for the selected Point of Interconnection (i) does not have any 
available bus positions and (ii) (a) is not expandable electrically or within the 
existing substation footprint, and (b) the Transmission Owner has determined that 
adjacent usable vacant land is not available, or 
(2) proposals by Eligible Customer are inconsistent with Good Utility Practice or 
Applicable Reliability Requirements; or 
(3) a viable tie line cable route(s) cannot be established from either the Point of 
Change of Ownership to the Point of Interconnection or, where these points are 
the same, a viable route cannot be established within or from the fence line; or 
(4) the project capacity exceeds the ratings of equipment at the substation selected 
for the Point of Interconnection, replacement equipment that would be adequately 
rated for the project capacity is not commercially available from an approved 
supplier and within applicable specifications set by the Transmission Owner, and 
an alternative upgrade is not physically feasible (e.g., higher voltage Point of 
Interconnection substation);251 or 
(5) an Attachment Facility or Local Network Upgrade required for the Large Load 
Project to reliably interconnect is Physically Infeasible.252 
Within 45 Calendar Days of the commencement of the RAMP Part 1 Process, the Connecting 
Transmission Owner or Affected Transmission Owner will provide the NYISO with its written 
assessment of the results of the Physical Infeasibility Screening.253  If, as a result of the Physical 
Infeasibility Screening or at any time in the Large Load Integration Study Process, the NYISO 
determines, in consultation with the Connecting Transmission Owner or Affected Transmission 
Owner, that the proposed interconnection of a Large Load Project is Physically Infeasible, then 
the NYISO will notify the Eligible Customer that the proposed interconnection of its Large Load 
Project is Physically Infeasible and will withdraw the Large Load Integration Request for the 
Large Load Project.254  However, in such circumstances, the Eligible Customer may modify its

b) 
NYISO Responsibilities in RAMP Part 1 Process 
The NYISO proposes as part of the RAMP Part 1 Process to establish the Pre-Project 
Base Case for purposes of the short-circuit/fault duty, steady state (thermal and voltage) and 
stability analyses for the System Impact Study.257  For this base case, the NYISO will use the 
most recent quarterly Short-Term Assessment of Reliability in its Short-Term Reliability Process 
established in Attachment FF of the NYISO OATT.258  As it is updated quarterly, the STAR base 
case represents an up-to-date set of planning cases for assessing reliability impacts.  The NYISO 
will then update the base case in the RAMP Part 2 Process to account for those Large Load 
Projects that elect in the SIS Entry Decision Period to enter the System Impact Study as 
described in Part II.H.vi.    
The NYISO will also conduct a Resource Adequacy Evaluation of each Large Load 
Project to evaluate the adverse impact of the interconnection of the Large Load Project and any 
Associated Resource identified in the Large Load Integration Request for the project on the 
resource adequacy of the New York Control Area.259  For this evaluation, the NYISO will apply 
the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard as described in Part II.G.ii to determine the preliminary 
amount of Firm Withdrawal Service and Non-Firm Withdrawal Service for the Large Load 
Project. 
Within 15 Calendar Days from the NYISO’s receipt of the results of the Physical 
Infeasibility Screening from all of the identified Connecting Transmission Owners and Affected 
Transmission Owners, the NYISO will issue a summary report with the results of the Resource

v. 
SIS Entry Decision Period 
The NYISO proposes to establish an SIS Entry Decision Period in which an Eligible 
Customer may assess the information determined in the RAMP Part 1 Process and elect whether 
its Large Load Project will proceed to be evaluated in a System Impact Study or be withdrawn 
from the Load Queue.261  The SIS Entry Decision Period will be a seven Calendar Day period, 
which period will commence 61 Calendar Days after the commencement of the RAMP Part 1 
Process.262 
For a Large Load Project to be included in the System Impact Study, the Eligible 
Customer must notify the NYISO of its election for the project to proceed to the study during the 
SIS Entry Decision Period.263  If the Eligible Customer does not make such election, then the 
NYISO will withdraw the project from the Load Queue without an opportunity to cure.264 
During the SIS Entry Decision Period, the Eligible Customer may propose two types of 
modifications to its proposed Large Load Project to account for input obtained in the RAMP Part 
1 Process.  First, the Eligible Customer may reduce the size (i.e., MW) of its Large Load Project 
from the size included in the Large Load Integration Request provided that the project with the 
reduced size continues to satisfy the definition of a Large Load Project.265  Second, the Eligible 
Customer may modify the Point of Interconnection included in its Large Load Integration 
Request to address Physical Infeasibility if such modification is agreed upon by the Connecting 
Transmission Owner and does not modify the electrical characteristics of the Large Load 
Project.266 
vi. 
RAMP Part 2 Process 
The NYISO proposes to establish a RAMP Part 2 Process prior to the System Impact 
Study to update materials based on the results of the SIS Entry Decision Period and to meet with 
the projects that elected to proceed to the System Impact Study.267  The scheduled duration of the 
Ramp Part 2 Process will be 23 Calendar Days.268

a) 
Performance of System Impact Study  
The NYISO proposes that the System Impact Study be performed on a combined basis 
for the Group of Large Load Projects that satisfied the requirements to proceed to the study.279  
The performance of the System Impact Study will be divided between the NYISO and the 
applicable Transmission Owner(s), with the NYISO performing analyses for the Bulk Electric 
System, and the applicable Transmission Owner performing analyses on the non-Bulk Electric 
System. 
In particular, the NYISO will perform short-circuit/fault duty, steady state (thermal and 
voltage) and stability analyses for the Bulk Electric System pursuant to the scope of the System 
Impact Study and the Transmission Security Standard.280  The NYISO will provide the study 
results to the applicable Transmission Owner(s) within 45 Calendar Days from the 
commencement of the System Impact Study.281  The applicable Transmission Owner(s) will 
perform these analyses for the non-Bulk Electric System and may perform bus flow analysis on 
the Bulk Electric System and non-Bulk Electric System.282  The applicable Transmission 
Owner(s) will provide the results of its analyses to the NYISO within 30 Calendar Days from the 
NYISO’s provision of the Bulk Electric System results.283   
Within the same 30-Calendar Day period the impacted Connecting Transmission 
Owner(s) and Affected Transmission Owner(s) will preliminarily identify Network Upgrades 
required to mitigate any transmission security impacts resulting from the proposed 
interconnection of the Large Load Project(s).284  The applicable Transmission Owner will 
provide preliminary, non-binding estimates of the costs and the time to construct the Network 
Upgrades.285  In addition, the applicable Transmission Owner will provide any updates to the

The NYISO proposes that the Eligible Customer must satisfy the following requirements 
during the ALI Entry Decision Period for its Large Load Project to be included in the 
Assessment of Load Integration and Facilities Study.298  If the Eligible Customer does not satisfy 
these requirements, then the NYISO will withdraw the project from the Load Queue without an 
opportunity to cure.299 
First, the NYISO proposes that the Eligible Customer must notify the NYISO of its 
election for its Large Load Project to proceed to the Assessment of Load Integration.300  Second, 
the NYISO proposes that the Eligible Customer must also satisfy a readiness requirement to 
proceed.  In particular, the Eligible Customer must submit a Readiness Deposit for its Large 
Load Project, which will evidence its commercial readiness to proceed.301  The NYISO proposes 
to calculate the Readiness Deposit as $4,000 per MW based on the Large Load Project’s peak 
MW amount.302  As described in the Nguyen Affidavit, this is consistent with the per MW 
amount for the Readiness Deposit 1 required in the NYISO’s Standard Interconnection 
Procedures previously accepted by the Commission.303 
Third, except for participants in the Transition Large Load Integration Study Process, the 
Eligible Customer must submit to the NYISO the Study Deposit 2 to account for the additional 
study work for the Assessment of Load Integration.304  The Study Deposit 2 will be the 
incremental difference between the Study Deposit 1 amount that the Eligible Customer 
previously submitted for the Large Load Project and the following amount based on the size of 
the Large Load Project: (i) $200,000 for a Large Load Project less than 200 MW, (ii) $250,000 
for a Large Load Project that is both less than 500 MW and greater than or equal to 200 MW, 
and (iii) $300,000 for a Large Load Project that is 500 MW or greater.305  The Study Deposit 1 
amount for the Large Load Project and the incremental difference submitted by the Eligible 
Customer for the ALI Entry Decision Period will, in total, constitute the Study Deposit 2 for the

The NYISO proposes to use for the base cases of the Assessment of Load Integration, as 
applicable, the base cases described in Parts II.G.ii and II.G.iii above for the performance of the 
Resource Adequacy Impact Standard and Transmission Security Standard.316  The NYISO 
proposes that it may update the assumptions in these base cases, including any updates received 
from Connecting Transmission Owners and Affected Transmission Owners on the Large Load 
Projects and associated Network Upgrades that have been studied in a previous cycle of the 
Large Load Integration Study Process, in accordance with the NYISO’s procedures.317  Within 5 
Business Days following the completion of the ALI Entry Decision Period, the Connecting 
Transmission Owners and Affected Transmission Owners will provide any updates for the 
NYISO to review and incorporate in the assumptions in the base cases.318  In addition, the 
NYISO will develop the scope of the ALI Part 1 Study, which it will provide to the impacted 
Connecting Transmission Owners and Affected Transmission Owners for their review.319  The 
NYISO will also provide the base cases that it will use for the Assessment of Load Integration to 
the impacted Connecting Transmission Owners and Affected Transmission Owners.320 
b) 
ALI Part 1 Study 
The NYISO proposes for the ALI Part 1 Study that it will evaluate the impact on the Bulk 
Electric System of the proposed interconnection of the Large Load Projects that satisfied the 
requirements to enter the Assessment of Load Integration.321  For this evaluation, the NYISO 
will use: (i) the Resource Adequacy Impact Standard, as applicable, for the individual Large 
Load Projects (except for State Exempt Large Load Projects) and for the collective group of any 
State Exempt Large Load Projects and (ii) the Transmission Security Standard for the Group of 
Large Load Projects, including confirming each Large Load Project’s contribution to any 
observed transmission security violations.322 
Within 150 Calendar Days from the end of the ALI Entry Decision Period, the NYISO 
will provide the applicable Transmission Owners with any transmission security violations 
identified in the ALI Part 1 Study for use by the Transmission Owner in the Facilities Study to

Based on the evaluations in the ALI Part 1 Study, the NYISO will update the amount of 
Firm Withdrawal Service and Non-Firm Withdrawal Service for each Large Load Project and, if 
requested, whether any portion of a Large Load Project is eligible to be classified as a Zero 
Withdrawal Service Load.325  The NYISO will provide the draft study results to Eligible 
Customers and Transmission Owners for their review.326  This will include: (i) the violations 
identified in the ALI Part 1 Study, (ii) the Large Load Project’s contribution percentages to the 
identified violations, (iii) the amount of Firm Withdrawal Service and Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service for each Large Load Project; and (iv) if requested, whether any portion of the Large 
Load Project is eligible to be classified as a Zero Withdrawal Service Load.327   
The NYISO will also perform sensitivities based on its engineering judgment to identify 
how the violations and contribution percentages determined in ALI Part 1 could change if one or 
more Large Load Projects withdraw, elect to obtain Non-Firm Withdrawal Service in lieu of 
Network Upgrades, or fail to accept its cost allocation and post security in accordance with the 
Facilities Study requirements.328  The NYISO will provide the Transmission Owners with the 
results of any sensitivities performed by the NYISO in ALI Part 1 for use by the Transmission 
Owner in the Facilities Study.329 
c) 
ALI Part 2 Study 
For the ALI Part 2 Study, the NYISO will review and confirm that the Non-Local 
Network Upgrades designed by Transmission Owners in the Facilities Studies resolve the 
violation(s) identified in the ALI Part 1 Study and do not result in new violations.330  If a 
Transmission Owner in a Facilities Study identifies the need for an additional Non-Local 
Network Upgrade or a Local Network Upgrade that the Transmission Owner believes impacts 
the Bulk Electric System, the NYISO will review and confirm that such Network Upgrade does 
not result in new violations on the Bulk Electric System.  The NYISO will notify the applicable 
Transmission Owner of the NYISO’s review within 30 Calendar Days of the NYISO’s receipt of 
the upgrade identified by the Transmission Owner.331

The NYISO proposes that the Facilities Studies for a Large Load Project be performed, 
as applicable, by the Connecting Transmission Owner to which system the project proposes to 
interconnect and any Affected Transmission Owner which system is impacted by the project.332  
Consistent with the NYISO’s existing load interconnection process and the NYISO’s other 
interconnection procedures, the Transmission Owner is responsible for determining the required 
Attachment Facilities and Network Upgrades for its system and estimating the costs and 
schedule for constructing such facilities.333  As described below, in the Facilities Study, the 
Transmission Owner will further determine the Attachment Facilities and Network Upgrades 
required for the reliable interconnection of the Large Load Project, identify the estimated costs 
and schedule to construct these facilities, and allocate the cost of the facilities among the 
applicable Large Load Projects.  The Transmission Owner will commence the Facilities Study 
for a Large Load Project no later than the NYISO’s commencement of the Assessment of Load 
Integration for the project.334 
The Transmission Owner will tender the Eligible Customer a Facilities Study 
agreement.335  In the agreement, the Eligible Customer must agree to reimburse the Transmission 
Owner(s) for performing the required Facilities Study and provide the study deposit required by 
the Transmission Owner.336  The Eligible Customer must execute a Facilities Study agreement, 
comply with any requirements of the Transmission Owner(s), and return the agreement to the 
Transmission Owner(s).337  The Transmission Owners will include the pro forma Facilities Study 
agreement in their separate Section 205 filing(s). 
a) 
Performance of Facilities Study 
The Connecting Transmission Owner will coordinate with any Affected Transmission 
Owners and Affected System Operators concerning the performance of the Facilities Studies.338 
The applicable Transmission Owner may group Large Load Projects as appropriate for the 
performance of the Facility Study.339 
The Connecting Transmission Owner and Affected Transmission Owner will design the 
Attachment Facilities and Local Network Upgrades required to reliably interconnect the Large 
Load Project with the New York State Transmission System in accordance with Applicable 
Reliability Requirements, Applicable Laws and Regulations, and Good Utility Practice.340  The

b) 
Consideration of Alternative Transmission Technologies 
In the Show Cause Order, the Commission directed the NYISO to propose tariff revisions 
to require (1) the evaluation of alternative transmission technologies as potential solutions to 
accommodate an Eligible Customer’s request for transmission service on behalf of a large load, 
and (2) if alternative transmission technologies are not identified, to include in the study report a 
sufficiently clear demonstration of why alternative transmission technologies are not feasible or 
would not result in lower costs or a faster timeline for accommodating the transmission service 
request.348 
The NYISO proposes that the Transmission Owner consider in identifying the Network 
Upgrades in the Facilities Study the use of static synchronous compensators, static VAR 
compensators, advanced power flow control devices, transmission switching, synchronous 
condensers, voltage source converters, advanced conductors, and tower lifting.349  The 
Transmission Owner will consider each alternative transmission technology that could 
reasonably be implemented to address the identified violation or constraint and determine 
whether the above technologies should be used, consistent with Good Utility Practice, 
Applicable Reliability Requirements, and Applicable Laws and Regulations.350  The 
Transmission Owner will provide an explanation of the results for each technology.351 
c) 
Completion of Facilities Study 
At the conclusion of the Facilities Study, the Connecting Transmission Owner and 
Affected Transmission Owner will require the Large Load Project to accept the cost allocation of 
any required Attachment Facilities and Network Upgrades and to post security to cover the 
allocated cost, consistent with the terms of the Transmission Owner’s cost recovery agreement 
and security agreement.352  If the Large Load Project does not satisfy a milestone demonstrating 
its acceptance of its cost responsibility for Network Upgrades and Attachment Facilities in 
accordance with the requirements established by the applicable Transmission Owner within 14 
Calendar Days of the completion of the Facilities Study, the applicable Transmission Owner will 
notify the NYISO, and the NYISO will withdraw the Large Load Project from the Load 
Queue.353 
The Connecting Transmission Owner and Affected Transmission Owner will inform the 
NYISO of the results of the Facilities Study, including: (i) any Network Upgrades identified for 
each Large Load Project and their estimated cost and time to construct and enter service, and (ii)

iii. 
Conclusion of Large Load Integration Study Process 
In the Show Cause Order, the Commission indicated that the NYISO’s current tariff 
appears unjust and unreasonable because it does not require the provision of data on NYISO’s 
website in a single location and in an easily accessible format that is searchable and allows users 
to filter the data that identifies the Network Upgrades that are needed to accommodate requests 
for transmission service by Eligible Customers on behalf of large loads, and the costs of those 
Network Upgrades.355   
The NYISO proposes to provide details on its website concerning the results of the Large 
Load Integration Study Process following its completion of the Assessment of Load Integration 
and the Transmission Owners’ provision to the NYISO of the final results of the Facilities 
Studies.  In particular, the NYISO will notify stakeholders of the completion of the Large Load 
Integration Study Process for the Group of Large Load Projects.  The NYISO will post on its 
website a summary in an easily accessible format that is searchable and allows users to filter the 
data that provides: (i) each Large Load Project’s final amount of Firm Withdrawal Service and 
Non-Firm Withdrawal Service and, if requested, its eligibility to be classified as a Zero 
Withdrawal Service Load, (ii) the Network Upgrades identified for each Large Load Project as 
provided by the Connecting Transmission Owner and/or Affected Transmission Owner, and (iii) 
the cost of the Network Upgrades.356   
The NYISO proposes that after its final reconciliation of the Eligible Customer’s costs 
incurred in the Large Load Integration Study Process and Eligible Customer’s payment of all 
invoices, the NYISO will proceed with the return and cancellation process for the Study Deposit 
and Readiness Deposit as described in Part III.B.i.c.357 
J. 
Affected Systems 
The NYISO proposes tariff revisions in Attachment LL to address how Affected System 
impacts of load interconnections will be assessed within the New York Control Area and how 
the NYISO will coordinate with neighboring regions – External Affected Systems – concerning 
the impacts of load interconnections.

Coordination with Affected Systems within the New York Control Area 
The NYISO proposes to coordinate the conduct of any studies required to determine the 
impact of the Large Load Integration Request on Affected Systems within the New York Control

ii. 
Coordination with External Affected Systems 
The NYISO proposes to establish requirements concerning how it will coordinate with its 
neighboring regions – External Affected Systems – concerning the coordination and study of the 
impacts of load projects proposing to interconnect in New York or in a neighboring region.363  
The NYISO’s proposal to establish separate requirements for coordination with External 
Affected Systems is consistent with its approach in its Standard Interconnection Procedures. 
For  Large Load Projects proposing to interconnect in the NYISO’s region, the NYISO 
proposes to identify potential impacts on External Affected Systems during the Resource 
Adequacy and Modeling Period once the Large Load Projects participating in the Group for the 
Large Load Integration Study Process have been confirmed.364  At the time of initial notification, 
the NYISO will provide an impacted Eligible Customer with a list of potential Affected Systems, 
along with relevant contact information for such systems.365  The NYISO will coordinate the 
conduct of any studies required to determine the impact of the Large Load Integration Request 
on External Affected Systems with External Affected System Operators.366 Eligible Customer 
will cooperate with the NYISO and External Affected System Operator in all matters related to

iii. 
Study of Impacts to New York State Transmission System of an Affected 
System Eligible Customer’s Proposed Interconnection to Another Region’s 
Transmission System 
The NYISO proposes to establish its responsibilities when it receives notification that a 
customer in another region – an Affected System Eligible Customer – is proposing to 
interconnect a load project to another region’s transmission system that may impact the New 
York State Transmission System.370  In such case, the NYISO will cooperate with the other 
region in all matters related to the conduct of studies and the determination of modifications to 
the New York State Transmission System.371 
a) 
Response to Notification  
When the NYISO receives initial notification based on the applicable interconnection 
study or restudy in another region that an Affected System Eligible Customer’s proposed 
interconnection of a load project to another region’s transmission system may impact the New 
York State Transmission System, the NYISO must respond in writing within 20 Business Days 
whether it intends to conduct an Affected System Load Study.372  By 15 Business Days after the 
NYISO responds with its affirmative intent to conduct an Affected System Load Study, the 
NYISO will share with Affected System Eligible Customer(s) and its host region a non-binding 
good faith estimate of the cost and the schedule to complete the Affected System Load Study.373 
b) 
Affected System Load Queue Position

The NYISO proposes to assign an Affected System Load Queue Position to Affected

c) 
Affected System Load Study Agreement

System Load Study Agreement or a Multiparty Affected System Load Study Agreement, as 
applicable, within 10 Business Days of the NYISO sharing the schedule for the Affected System 
Load Study, unless otherwise agreed upon.379  The NYISO proposes to establish these form study 
agreements in Appendices 4 and 5 of Attachment LL.  The study agreements are consistent with 
the agreements used in the NYISO’s Standard Interconnection Procedures.  The NYISO will 
invoice the Affected System Eligible Customer(s) for the actual cost of the Affected System 
Load Study, and the NYISO will notify Affected System Eligible Customer’s host region of any 
failure to pay.380

the executed agreement to the NYISO, and provide the study deposit in the amount of $100,000 
within 10 Business Days of receipt.381  If the Affected System Eligible Customer does not 
provide all required technical data when it delivers the study agreement, the NYISO will notify 
the deficient Affected System Eligible Customer, as well as its host region, of the technical data 
deficiency within 10 Business Days of the receipt of the executed study agreement.382  The 
deficient Affected System Eligible Customer must cure the technical data deficiency within 10

d) 
Scope of Affected System Load Study 
For the Affected System Load Study, the NYISO proposes to use the most recent of the 
base case of the latest in time Assessment of Load Integration or Short-Term Reliability Process 
that is available at the time of the commencement of the Affected System Load Study.386  The 
NYISO will coordinate with the Affected System Eligible Customer(s)’ host region as necessary 
to align to the extent possible the network system modeling between the regions for purposes of 
the Affected System Load Study.387 
The NYISO will identify and notify any Transmission Owners whose systems are 
potentially impacted by the Affected System Eligible Customer’s load project.388  The NYISO 
and the identified Transmission Owners will develop the scope of the Affected System Load 
Study.389  The NYISO will hold a scoping meeting among the NYISO, the Affected System 
Eligible Customer(s) with load projects participating in the Group for the Affected System Load 
Study, and the impacted Transmission Owner(s).390  The NYISO will conduct the Affected 
System Load Study for a group of load projects and shall use existing studies to the extent 
practicable, when multiple Affected System Eligible Customers that are part of a single group 
may cause the need for Affected System Network Upgrades.391  The Affected System Load 
Study will commence upon agreement between the NYISO and Transmission Owner(s) 
concerning the scope of the study.392 
The NYISO will evaluate the impact that Affected System Eligible Customer’s proposed 
interconnection of a load project to another region’s transmission system will have on the 
reliability of the Bulk Electric System portion of the New York State Transmission System using 
the Transmission Security Standard.393  The Transmission Owner(s) will evaluate the impact of 
the interconnection of the Affected System Eligible Customer’s load project on the non-Bulk 
Electric System using the Transmission Security Standard.394  If adverse impacts to reliability are 
identified, the NYISO, in coordination with the Transmission Owner, will identify potential 
Affected System Network Upgrades, to the extent not Physically Infeasible, and the

System Eligible Customer(s), and Affected Transmission Owner or Affected System Operator 
will meet as permitted by schedule or availability of the parties to discuss the results of the 
Affected System Load Study.397  Upon request, the NYISO will provide Affected System 
Eligible Customer(s) with all supporting documentation, workpapers and relevant power flow, 
short circuit and stability databases for the Affected System Load Study, subject to 
confidentiality arrangements.398 
e) 
Evaluation of Affected System Network Upgrades 
The NYISO proposes to establish requirements for the circumstance in which an Affected 
System Load Study identifies that Affected System Network Upgrades are required to mitigate 
adverse impacts to reliability of an Affected System Eligible Customer’s load project.  In such 
case, the NYISO proposes that the Affected System Eligible Customer must submit a 
Transmission Interconnection Application in accordance with the requirements in the NYISO’s 
Transmission Interconnection Procedures in Attachment P to the OATT for the evaluation of the 
identified Affected System Network Upgrades.399  This is consistent with the NYISO’s current 
practice for addressing impacts in New York of load projects proposing to interconnect in 
neighboring regions.   
The Affected System Network Upgrades may proceed directly to be evaluated in the 
Facilities Study in the Transmission Interconnection Procedures.400  The Affected System 
Eligible Customer must satisfy the requirements in Attachment P of the ISO OATT concerning 
the Affected System Network Upgrades, including accepting the Affected System Eligible 
Customer’s cost allocation identified in Attachment P and paying cash or posting Security for the 
allocated amount.401  The Affected Transmission Owner or Affected System Operator must enter 
into a construction agreement with the Affected System Eligible Customer for the construction 
of the Affected System Network Upgrades.402

POST-STUDY REQUIREMENTS

The NYISO proposes that following the completion of the Facilities Study the Eligible 
Customer for the Large Load Project may elect to continue with the proposed interconnection of 
its Large Load Project by (i) entering into an interconnection agreement with the Transmission 
Owner for which system the Eligible Customer proposed to interconnect its Large Load Project 
and (ii), as applicable, entering into construction agreement(s) for Network Upgrades with any 
Transmission Owner(s) or Affected System Operator(s) for which system a Network Upgrade is 
required in connection with the Large Load Project.403 The agreements must identify any 
required Attachment Facilities and Network Upgrades and specify the Large Load Project’s cost 
responsibility for such facilities consistent with the terms of the applicable Transmission 
Owner’s cost recovery agreement and security agreement.404  The NYISO proposes that these 
agreements remain two-party agreements between the Transmission Owner and the Large Load 
Project consistent with the longstanding practice under its existing load interconnection 
requirements.  The Transmission Owner is responsible for determining the Attachment Facilities 
and Network Upgrades required for the safe and reliable interconnection of the Large Load 
Project, estimating and allocating the costs of these facilities, and owns and/or operates the 
system impacted by the interconnection, while the Large Load Project is responsible for paying 
for the expense of the required facilities and for coordinating with the Transmission Owner 
concerning the construction of the facilities.  As discussed in Part VI below, the NYISO 
anticipates that the Transmission Owners will include two-party security, cost recovery, and 
interconnection agreements in their concurrent FPA Section 205 filing.  
ii. 
Transmission Service Agreement for Firm and Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service for Large Loads 
In the Show Cause Order, the Commission preliminarily found the NYISO’s tariff to be 
unjust and unreasonable because it lacks pro forma provisions in a transmission service 
agreement between NYISO and the transmission customer taking transmission service on behalf 
of a large load to memorialize operational requirements for transmission customers taking 
transmission service on behalf of large loads (e.g., equipment requirements, data requirements, 
remote disconnect capability).405   
The NYISO proposes to establish in its OATT requirements for entering into a 
Transmission Service Agreement and the proposed pro forma Transmission Service Agreement. 
a) 
Process for Entering into Transmission Service Agreement 
The NYISO proposes to require the Eligible Customer and the Large Load (if the Large 
Load is not its own Eligible Customer or Load Serving Entity) to enter into a Transmission 
Service Agreement with the NYISO.406  The NYISO will offer the standard form Transmission 
Service Agreement to an Eligible Customer when it submits a Completed Application, and the

Pro Forma Transmission Service Agreement  
The NYISO proposes to establish a new, pro forma Transmission Service Agreement for

The NYISO proposes to establish certain general requirements in the Transmission 
Service Agreement.409  In particular, the Transmission Service Agreement establishes that the 
NYISO agrees to provide, and the Transmission Customer agrees to pay, for Firm Withdrawal 
Service and/or Non-Firm Withdrawal Service in accordance with the Withdrawal Service 
requirements in the OATT, other Transmission Owner rate schedules, and the terms of the 
Transmission Service Agreement.410  The agreement specifies that the Transmission Customer’s

Large Load Integration Procedures in Attachment LL and other applicable requirements in the 
OATT and Services Tariff.411  The agreement provides that such Withdrawal Service is subject

requirements in the Services Tariff, and the NYISO’s procedures.412 
The agreement provides that the Withdrawal Service provided under the agreement will 
commence on the latest of: (1) the requested service commencement date, (2) the date 
construction of any Attachment Facilities and/or Network Upgrades are completed, (3) the date 
that all of the applicable preconditions for obtaining the requested Withdrawal Service set forth 
in the tariffs are satisfied, or (4) any such date the Withdrawal Service is permitted to commence 
by the Commission.413 
The NYISO also proposes to include additional requirements in the Transmission Service 
Agreement that are specific to the Firm Withdrawal Service and/or the Non-Firm Withdrawal 
Service for the Large Load.

Transmission Customer and Large Load comply with all applicable requirements in the OATT

iii. 
Large Load Performance, Protection and Control, and Monitoring 
Requirements 
In the Show Cause Order, the Commission preliminarily found the NYISO’s tariff to be 
unjust and unreasonable because it lacks provisions that address ongoing operational 
requirements for transmission customers serving large loads necessary to ensure reliable 
operation of the transmission system.416  The NYISO proposes to establish operational 
requirements for Large Load in its tariffs as described below and in the Nguyen Affidavit.417  In 
particular, the NYISO proposes that a Large Load that was subject to the Large Load Integration 
Procedures must comply with Applicable Reliability Standards, including providing to the 
NYISO and applicable Transmission Owner(s) all information that they may reasonably require 
to comply with Applicable Laws and Regulations and Applicable Reliability Standards.418  In 
addition, the NYISO proposes that the Large Load must satisfy performance, protection and 
control, and monitoring requirements described below that will be included in the OATT and 
further detailed in the NYISO’s procedures, unless the NYISO agrees to an exemption to a 
requirement.419  The NYISO proposes to explicitly require in the Transmission Service

Agreement that the Large Load must comply with these performance, protection and control, and 
monitoring requirements.420 
The NYISO proposes performance, protection and control, and operating requirements as 
detailed below that address the Commission’s findings in the Show Cause Order (which 
requirements identified with an asterisk are eligible for an exemption as described below).   
First, the Commission found that the NYISO’s existing tariff does not specify the ramp 
rate or ride-through requirements with which the transmission customer taking transmission 
service on behalf of the large load must comply.421  The NYISO proposes to include in 
Attachment LL that a Large Load must satisfy the following categories of performance 
requirements, which will be detailed in the NYISO’s procedures: (i) active power ramp rate*, (ii) 
active power recovery*, (iii) high and low voltage ride through*, (iv) high and low frequency 
ride through*, (v) multi-disturbance ride through*, (vi) stable operation, (vii) torsional and 
controller interaction, (viii) cycling, (ix) reactive power capability requirement*, and (x) voltage 
phase jump*.422   
Second, the Commission found that the NYISO’s existing tariff does not: (i) specify that 
transmission customer enable the NYISO to remotely disconnect large loads when necessary to 
maintain reliability of the transmission system, with the costs of such equipment directly 
assigned to the transmission customer, as well as the terms and conditions under which NYISO 
may remotely disconnect the large load (e.g., emergency conditions, so that transmission 
equipment is not harmed) or (ii) require necessary control technologies and/or protection systems 
that may be necessary to limit a transmission customer’s withdrawals from the transmission 
system as appropriate.423  The NYISO proposes to include in Attachment LL that a Large Load 
must satisfy the following protection and control requirements: (i) remote disconnect ability by 
the Connecting Transmission Owner, (ii) active-power variation monitoring relays,* and (iii) 
injection and withdrawal limits for co-located facilities implemented with appropriate 
controls.424 
Finally, the Commission identified that the NYISO’s existing tariff does not specify that: 
(i) transmission customer provide hourly forecasts, telemetry data, and other data to ensure 
sufficient visibility or specify requirements for maintaining communication channels between 
NYISO and the transmission customer on behalf of the large load, or (ii) that the transmission 
owner install, and transmission customer pay for, equipment to enable the NYISO to monitor 
large loads for impacts of, for example, fast-ramping load that may impact the transmission 
system too quickly to be captured by conventional data acquisition systems (e.g., the need for 
phasor measurement units or similar equipment).425  The NYISO proposes to include in 
Attachment LL that a Large Load must possess the following minimum telemetry requirements 
to ensure sufficient visibility concerning the Large Load: (i) phase 1 analog metering, (ii)

revenue grade metering, (iii) SCADA MW/MVAR metering, (iv) digital fault recorder (“DFR”), 
(v) phasor measurement unit (“PMU”) installations, (vi) sequence of event recorder (“SER”), 
and (vii) dynamic disturbance recorder (“DDR”).426  The communications equipment will 
facilitate the provision of the data to the NYISO identified in the Show Cause Order, with the 
exception of load forecast data, which reporting requirements will be addressed in accordance 
with updated NYISO procedures. 
The NYISO proposes that an Eligible Customer for a Large Load that is a Non-
Computational Large Load may request that the NYISO grant an exemption from a performance 
or a protection and control requirement that is eligible for exemption.427  The operational 
characteristics of Non-Computational Large Loads, such as traditional manufacturing facilities, 
are driven by the physical attributes of the load and may not be able to meet certain of the 
performance or protection and control requirements (e.g., ramp rates that do not align with a 
reasonable operation of the facility). 
The Eligible Customer for a Non-Computational Large Load may request the exemption 
as part of its Large Load Integration Request or through a modification request.428  To request an 
exemption, the Eligible Customer must identify the requirement for which it is requesting an 
exemption, provide justification for why the requirement cannot be met, and propose an 
alternative to the requirement.429  If the request is part of a modification request, the Eligible 
Customer must also provide technical rationale to support that the proposed alternative would 
not adversely impact the reliability of the New York State Transmission System.430 The NYISO 
or the applicable Transmission Owner may perform studies, at Eligible Customer’s expense, to 
determine if the proposed alternative is permissible. The NYISO will not grant an exemption if it 
is determined to create reliability concerns or a reliability violation.431

Owner(s) and any Affected Transmission Owner(s) must report on the status of the Large Load
